./DECISIONS.md:11:6. **Selection and uncertainty.** Per-feature-set winners are selected using the same saved folds that are reported. These are exploratory model comparisons, not a nested estimate of the selection procedure. Fold sample SD is descriptive, not a confidence interval or a significance test. A tiny MAE difference is not evidence of equivalence. No new inferential p-values are introduced for model comparisons.
./DECISIONS.md:24:16. **Paired diagnostic.** Add a saved-prediction reanalysis with Ridge fixed for metadata and metadata+HSV. Pair all 96 specimen/split keys, average two absolute errors per specimen, and then weight the 48 specimens equally. HSV improves 19 and worsens 29; mean change is +0.000218646 kN and median +0.009439030 kN. Positive means worse with HSV. These are descriptive results after selection, not independent repeats, a significance test, or equivalence evidence. Their weighting differs from the primary mean of fold scores.

./main/main.tex:41:The repository contains strong current-state evidence for visible corrosion quantification and weaker, more assumption-heavy evidence for metadata-driven progression and threshold timing. It also contains substantial reproducibility drift: the current raw workbook no longer matches the identifier and column conventions expected by the code, the image-feature cache is keyed by row-level IDs that the current loader no longer reconstructs, and the deployment manifest stores absolute artifact paths on another machine. This report therefore distinguishes carefully between what the saved artifacts support, what the present code still implements in principle, and what can no longer be rerun from the current raw files without repair.

./report_v2/article/v3/article.tex:29:Atha and Jahanshahi study convolutional-network corrosion detection \cite{atha2018}; Spencer et al. review vision-based inspection and monitoring \cite{spencer2019}. Detecting surface evidence does not itself validate structural-capacity prediction. For structured datasets, Roberts et al. discuss blocking and restricted predictor support \cite{roberts2017}, while Kapoor and Narayanan examine leakage and resulting scientific claims \cite{kapoor2023}. These motivate specimen grouping and explicit prediction timing. Cawley and Talbot identify bias from finite-sample model selection \cite{cawley2010}; our selected-model comparisons are therefore exploratory, not nested evaluations. This focused review does not establish exhaustive novelty.
./report_v2/article/v3/article.tex:68:Historical experiments using classical colour features, frozen image embeddings, and interpretable descriptors demonstrate visible-corrosion signal under grouped specimen evaluation. They use partly different dataset and label schemas and are not ranked as one benchmark here. Their agreement is evidence from distinct representations on overlapping specimens, not independent experimental replication.
./report_v2/article/v3/article.tex:70:A more specific limit appears in the refined total-rust audit. ./report_v2/thesis/v3/chapters/06_structural_results.tex:60:This is evidence of sensitivity within the saved sample, not a forecast of the benefit of collecting more specimens. ./report_v2/thesis/v3/chapters/06_structural_results.tex:81:The structural evidence converges on a limited capability: pooled prediction can exploit specimen and exposure metadata, while incremental image value and transfer are not established reliably. ./main_4_old/main.tex:55:That wording matters. The codebase does not claim that the dataset supports direct image-to-RUL learning. ./main_4_old/main.tex:74:    \item robust, explainable baseline evidence is needed before stronger modelling claims can be justified.
./main_4_old/main.tex:238:\textbf{Input.} Parsed metadata from the workbook audit stage. \textbf{Output.} An enriched master table with validated specimen mapping, cleaned numeric columns, and structural-row flags. \textbf{Purpose.} This step turns the workbook into a modelling-ready longitudinal table without losing audit traceability. \textbf{Key design choices.} A deterministic mapping file, \texttt{configs/specimen\_mapping.yaml}, reconstructs \texttt{campaign\_id}, \texttt{series\_id}, \texttt{split\_group\_treatment}, \texttt{treatment\_protocol}, terminal duration, and campaign-linked specimen attributes. The code also standardizes \texttt{cover\_mm}, forces \texttt{n\_steel\_mesh} to integer type, creates \texttt{has\_structural\_label}, and resolves a source inconsistency by treating the workbook field \texttt{Last\_Wire\_Area\_Loss\_(Faliure\_Surface)\_\%} as a fraction-valued variable, saved canonically as \texttt{wire\_area\_loss\_frac} and secondarily as \texttt{wire\_area\_loss\_pct}. \textbf{Residual limitations.} Campaign and treatment assignments are reconstructed from thesis evidence rather than directly encoded in the workbook, so the mapping is deterministic and reviewable but not fully self-contained in the raw data.

./main_4_old/main.tex:497:\textbf{Interpretation and evidence strength.} Figure~\ref{fig:collapse-heatmap} is strong evidence because it summarizes the central robustness lesson of the project. ./report_v2/thesis/v3/chapters/09_limitations.tex:2:The limitations are part of the central result because they determine which uses the evidence can support. This chapter groups them by the part of the inference chain they constrain.
./report_v2/thesis/v3/chapters/09_limitations.tex:15:Specimen-disjoint splits prevent a direct overlap of specimen histories, but they do not remove campaign confounding, target-adjacent features, or selection bias. Earlier image-fallback and imputation procedures are historical implementation limitations rather than evidence that all preprocessing was fitted independently in every old benchmark. The focused capacity implementation fits its preprocessing within training folds.
./report_v2/thesis/v3/chapters/09_limitations.tex:19:A negative mean $R^2$ is evidence of poor performance under that score and partitioning, not a theorem that every prediction is useless. Conversely, moderate pooled MAE does not establish calibration, useful ranking within a subgroup, or adequate performance for a structural decision.
./report_v2/thesis/v3/chapters/09_limitations.tex:37:Taken together, the limitations support a focused scientific conclusion rather than an operational predictor. The final chapter states what is completed, what remains prepared, and what additional evidence each stronger claim would require.
./main_2/main.tex:36:The repository also contains important reproducibility drift. The saved artifacts document one successful run on 792 observations, but the current raw Excel workbook now has two header rows, 791 labeled data rows, no directly exposed \texttt{NaCl\%} column, and a different identifier layout than the loader expects. The single corrupted image \texttt{E01-20240508-17W.png} is still present in the image directory and in the saved predictions, but its label row is absent from the current workbook. The report therefore treats the saved outputs as evidence of what was run, while explicitly marking what can no longer be rerun from the present raw files without code or data repair.

./main_2/main.tex:325:Numeric metadata & Median imputation and standardization of \texttt{N\_Steel\_Mesh}, \texttt{NaCl\%}, \texttt{Ageing\_Days}, \texttt{Cover\_(Faliure\_Surface)\_[mm]}, and \texttt{week}. & Encodes specimen design, exposure duration, saline regime, and cover geometry. These can correlate with corrosion because the experiment itself is structured by treatment and time. & Shortcut-learning risk is high: \texttt{week}, \texttt{NaCl\%}, and structural family can act as proxies for expected corrosion level rather than image evidence. In the current raw workbook, \texttt{NaCl\%} and \texttt{Ageing\_Days} are not directly reproducible under the names the code expects. \\

./report_v2/thesis/v3/chapters/10_status_conclusions.tex:39:The principal result is a calibrated boundary: photographic surface assessment is supported more directly than structural inference, and structural inference is supported more directly than lifetime prediction. By tying claims to target definitions, independent specimen counts, metadata controls, and evaluation regimes, the activity provides a defensible basis for the next experiment. The appendices preserve the implementation detail needed to inspect and reproduce that account.

./report_v2/thesis/v3/chapters/05_surface_results.tex:23:\fig{label_adjacency}{1}{Numerical alignment of the total surface-rust label and the rust-area feature on all 791 matched observations. The left panel compares the two percentages with an identity line; the right panel shows feature-minus-label residuals in percentage points. Spearman correlation is 0.9996. ./report_v2/thesis/v3/chapters/05_surface_results.tex:27:The refined near-identity result is useful as a measurement-consistency check. It is weak evidence for a broad claim that a model discovered a new relation between appearance and corrosion state. Learning a nearly equivalent feature-to-label mapping is different from recovering an independent physical target. The classical peak-rust benchmark has a related concern because a colour-mask feature dominates its importance ranking, but that is a different target and the available evidence does not establish the same numerical identity.
./report_v2/thesis/v3/chapters/05_surface_results.tex:42:Visible corrosion is consistently learnable within the saved experiments, but the strongest total-coverage result is almost reconstructive. Campaign-out surface results remain informative in both later representations; treatment-out results differ between them. The available evidence does not isolate whether representation, label/schema changes, or treatment grouping explains that disagreement. The defensible conclusion is phase-dependent treatment robustness, followed by a separate assessment of structural targets in the next chapter.

./report_v2/thesis/v3/chapters/08_discussion.tex:2:The evidence is most useful when interpreted as a hierarchy of questions with different supervision requirements. This chapter separates direct observations from plausible explanations and the research decisions they motivate.
./report_v2/thesis/v3/chapters/08_discussion.tex:7:It is tempting to conclude that sparse labels alone explain all structural weakness. The available experiments do not support that decomposition. The saved learning curve varies the number of training specimens, but subset composition changes with it. It does not separate sample size from design coverage or measure all potentially relevant physical factors. Surface appearance may omit information about local metal loss, bond, geometry, or cracking; the selected feature families may also fail to capture available information. The evidence identifies these as unresolved possibilities rather than estimated causes.
./report_v2/thesis/v3/chapters/08_discussion.tex:9:The same discipline applies to label adjacency. Near reconstruction explains why a particular total-rust benchmark needs modest interpretation. It does not erase the practical utility of automated surface measurement, and it does not make every peak-rust result tautological. Target-specific evidence should govern the scope of each concern.
./report_v2/thesis/v3/chapters/08_discussion.tex:28:These findings are mutually informative, but they are not all independent replications. Most share the same specimens; the full image/metadata ablation is centralized in one modelling generation. The report therefore treats agreement as convergence of diagnostics within a dataset, not evidence of broad external validity.
./report_v2/thesis/v3/chapters/01_introduction.tex:5:Ferrocement combines a cementitious matrix with distributed steel-mesh reinforcement. For the laboratory specimens considered here, external rust evidence can be recorded repeatedly while structural testing is destructive. This asymmetry makes visual monitoring attractive but complicates the interpretation of an image-based predictor. An accurate estimate of visible corrosion is not automatically an accurate estimate of lost wire area, residual capacity, or future service life.

This supports reproduction of the reported aggregation; it does not establish that the historically trained models can currently be regenerated without source repairs.
./report_v2/thesis/v3/chapters/01_introduction.tex:32:Chapter~\ref{ch:background} positions the study in imaging and evaluation research. Chapters~\ref{ch:data} and \ref{ch:methods} define observations and methods. Chapters~\ref{ch:surface}--\ref{ch:proxy} answer the research questions using saved evidence. The discussion and limitations then connect those observations to concrete research decisions. ./report_v2/thesis/v3/chapters/07_proxy_status.tex:11:A forward interval can be reported only when $t_i^{\ast}$ is later than the last observation and falls within the chosen projection horizon. Crossing before or during observation is a different status. Failure to cross within a finite projection is a model/horizon outcome, not evidence that the real specimen survives indefinitely.

./report_v2/thesis/v3/chapters/02_background.tex:12:Atha and Jahanshahi evaluate convolutional-network approaches to corrosion detection \citep{atha2018}. Their work motivates visual feature learning for detecting corrosion evidence. It does not establish that a detector's score estimates internal metal loss or terminal capacity in ferrocement. Those are different targets requiring their own ground truth and validation.
./report_v2/thesis/v3/chapters/02_background.tex:16:Frozen residual-network embeddings provide one intermediate representation between colour rules and fully task-specific deep learning. Residual networks were developed to facilitate the optimization of deep visual models \citep{he2016}; in this activity, an existing network supplies fixed image features and a separately trained regression head predicts the visible label. The saved experiment is evidence about that particular transfer-learning arrangement, not about the universal superiority of deep or handcrafted representations.
./report_v2/thesis/v3/chapters/02_background.tex:26:The evidence reviewed above motivates a specific contribution: testing whether visible-corrosion representations support incremental structural prediction under realistic information and grouping constraints. The specific question is incremental terminal-capacity estimation after accounting for metadata, rather than visual corrosion detection alone. The study supplies a bounded empirical case, not a new general-purpose model; the focused literature search does not establish an exhaustive novelty claim. Its value lies in explaining how apparently encouraging pooled scores change when the target, observation unit, and evaluation population are specified. The next chapter makes that observation structure explicit.

./main_4_old/figures.tex:43:The temporal figures are the strongest direct evidence that visible corrosion evolves over the observation window, but they also show that progression is heterogeneous and not strictly monotone for every specimen.
./main_4_old/figures.tex:74:The repository does not save intermediate segmentation masks or strip overlays as figures. The recoverable figure evidence for the image-processing stage is therefore downstream: feature-target associations and feature-feature redundancy.
./main_4_old/figures.tex:93:The top panel shows that mask ratios, grayscale statistics, rust-blob features, and strip summaries do not behave as independent signals. The bottom panel makes that explicit: the diagnostics tables contain 36 high-collinearity pairs, including exact or near-exact duplicates such as \texttt{ageing\_days} versus \texttt{week}, \texttt{img\_brightness\_std} versus \texttt{img\_contrast}, and \texttt{img\_rust\_area\_ratio\_pct} versus \texttt{img\_strip\_rust\_mean\_pct}. This is the available figure evidence for the spatial strip-based family: the repository exposes strip-derived summary variables such as mean, max, p90, standard deviation, and peak location, but it does not export a strip-by-strip corrosion map. Interpretation should therefore stay at the level of strip summary statistics rather than spatially resolved pit morphology.

./report_v2/thesis/v2/chapters/06_structural_results.tex:25:Adding HSV descriptors to metadata changes mean MAE only from 0.172711 to 0.173003\,kN while increasing mean rank correlation from 0.758 to 0.771. ./report_v2/thesis/v2/chapters/06_structural_results.tex:55:The structural evidence converges on a limited capability: pooled prediction can exploit specimen and exposure metadata, while incremental image value and transfer are not established reliably. The following chapter examines what happens when these structural estimates are propagated into time-dependent screening.
./report_v2/thesis/v2/chapters/00_abstract.tex:19:\textbf{What remains useful.} The work provides reproducible aggregation of saved results, specimen-disjoint evaluation manifests, a clear separation between observed and derived quantities, and a prepared classification dataset. Negative results identify precisely which claims require new evidence.
./report_v2/thesis/v2/chapters/09_limitations.tex:2:The limitations are part of the central result because they determine which uses the evidence can support. This chapter groups them by the part of the inference chain they constrain.
./report_v2/thesis/v2/chapters/09_limitations.tex:15:Specimen-disjoint splits prevent a direct overlap of specimen histories, but they do not remove campaign confounding, target-adjacent features, or selection bias. Earlier image-fallback and imputation procedures are historical implementation limitations rather than evidence that all preprocessing was fitted independently in every old benchmark. The focused capacity implementation fits its preprocessing within training folds.
./report_v2/thesis/v2/chapters/09_limitations.tex:19:A negative mean $R^2$ is evidence of poor performance under that score and partitioning, not a theorem that every prediction is useless. Conversely, moderate pooled MAE does not establish calibration, useful ranking within a subgroup, or adequate performance for a structural decision.
./report_v2/thesis/v2/chapters/09_limitations.tex:37:Taken together, the limitations support a focused scientific conclusion rather than an operational predictor. The final chapter states what is completed, what remains prepared, and what additional evidence each stronger claim would require.

Conversely, moderate pooled MAE does not establish calibration, useful ranking within a subgroup, or adequate performance for a structural decision.
./report_v2/thesis/chapters/09_limitations.tex:37:Taken together, the limitations support a focused scientific conclusion rather than an operational predictor. The final chapter states what is completed, what remains prepared, and what additional evidence each stronger claim would require.

./report_v2/thesis/chapters/10_status_conclusions.tex:2:The completed evidence supports a research synthesis and a bounded empirical claim. This chapter distinguishes that completed work from the prepared classification branch and from future experiments that require additional validation or data.
./report_v2/thesis/chapters/10_status_conclusions.tex:39:The principal result is a calibrated boundary: photographic surface assessment is supported more directly than structural inference, and structural inference is supported more directly than lifetime prediction. This hierarchy is an interpretation of the present evidence, not a universal ordering of possible methods. By tying claims to target definitions, independent specimen counts, metadata controls, and evaluation regimes, the activity provides a defensible basis for the next experiment. The appendices preserve the implementation detail needed to inspect and reproduce that account.
./report_v2/thesis/chapters/05_surface_results.tex:2:This chapter answers RQ1 and the surface component of RQ4. The evidence supports learnable visible corrosion, while also showing why a highly accurate visual benchmark must be checked for label adjacency.
./report_v2/thesis/chapters/05_surface_results.tex:23:\fig{label_adjacency}{1}{Numerical alignment of the total surface-rust label and the rust-area feature on all 791 matched observations. The left panel compares the two percentages with an identity line; the right panel shows feature-minus-label residuals in percentage points. Spearman correlation is 0.9996. This is evidence of near label reconstruction; it does not independently verify that identical code or thresholds created the original label.}{fig:adjacency}
./report_v2/thesis/chapters/05_surface_results.tex:25:This result is useful as a measurement-consistency check. It is weak evidence for a broad claim that a model discovered a new relation between appearance and corrosion state. Learning a nearly equivalent feature-to-label mapping is different from recovering an independent physical target. The classical peak-rust benchmark has a related concern because a colour-mask feature dominates its importance ranking, but that is a different target and the available evidence does not establish the same numerical identity.
./report_v2/thesis/chapters/05_surface_results.tex:39:Visible corrosion is consistently learnable within the saved experiments, but the strongest total-coverage result is almost reconstructive. Campaign-out surface results remain informative in both later representations; treatment-out results differ between them. The available evidence does not isolate whether representation, label/schema changes, or treatment grouping explains that disagreement. The defensible conclusion is phase-dependent treatment robustness, followed by a separate assessment of structural targets in the next chapter.
./report_v2/thesis/chapters/08_discussion.tex:2:The evidence is most useful when interpreted as a hierarchy of questions with different supervision requirements. This chapter separates direct observations from plausible explanations and the research decisions they motivate.
./report_v2/thesis/chapters/08_discussion.tex:7:It is tempting to conclude that sparse labels alone explain all structural weakness. The available experiments do not support that decomposition. They do not vary structural sample size independently of image representation, nor do they measure all potentially relevant physical factors. Surface appearance may omit information about local metal loss, bond, geometry, or cracking; the selected feature families may also fail to capture available information. The evidence identifies these as unresolved possibilities rather than estimated causes.
./report_v2/thesis/chapters/08_discussion.tex:9:The same discipline applies to label adjacency. Near reconstruction explains why a particular total-rust benchmark needs modest interpretation. It does not erase the practical utility of automated surface measurement, and it does not make every peak-rust result tautological. Target-specific evidence should govern the scope of each concern.
./report_v2/thesis/chapters/08_discussion.tex:26:These findings are mutually informative, but they are not all independent replications. Most share the same specimens; the full image/metadata ablation is centralized in one modelling generation. The report therefore treats agreement as convergence of diagnostics within a dataset, not evidence of broad external validity.
./report_v2/thesis/chapters/01_introduction.tex:5:Ferrocement combines a cementitious matrix with distributed steel-mesh reinforcement. For the laboratory specimens considered here, external rust evidence can be recorded repeatedly while structural testing is destructive. This asymmetry makes visual monitoring attractive but complicates the interpretation of an image-based predictor. An accurate estimate of visible corrosion is not automatically an accurate estimate of lost wire area, residual capacity, or future service life.
./report_v2/thesis/chapters/01_introduction.tex:12:The report organizes the evidence around five questions:
./report_v2/thesis/chapters/01_introduction.tex:27:The third contribution is a verification and reporting framework for the saved evidence. It checks specimen disjointness, reproduces key metrics from saved predictions, resolves conflicting model-selection summaries, and documents figure provenance. This supports reproduction of the reported aggregation; it does not establish that the historically trained models can currently be regenerated without source repairs.
./report_v2/thesis/chapters/01_introduction.tex:32:Chapter~\ref{ch:background} positions the study in imaging and evaluation research. Chapters~\ref{ch:data} and \ref{ch:methods} define observations and methods. Chapters~\ref{ch:surface}--\ref{ch:proxy} answer the research questions using saved evidence. The discussion and limitations then connect those observations to concrete research decisions. Reproduction details are confined to Appendix~\ref{app:reproduction} so the scientific narrative remains readable.
./report_v2/thesis/chapters/07_proxy_status.tex:11:A forward interval can be reported only when $t_i^{\ast}$ is later than the last observation and falls within the chosen projection horizon. Crossing before or during observation is a different status. Failure to cross within a finite projection is a model/horizon outcome, not evidence that the real specimen survives indefinitely.
./report_v2/thesis/chapters/07_proxy_status.tex:16:The refined curve-selection output contains 48 fits: 27 linear and 21 monotone-isotonic. Smoothness or monotonicity is a modelling constraint, not evidence that the inferred path reproduces the specimen's actual hidden degradation. The structural models were refitted on available structural data before generating these trajectories; the saved degradation inputs are not fully out-of-fold structural predictions.
./report_v2/thesis/chapters/02_background.tex:12:Atha and Jahanshahi evaluate convolutional-network approaches to corrosion detection \citep{atha2018}. Their work motivates visual feature learning for detecting corrosion evidence. It does not establish that a detector's score estimates internal metal loss or terminal capacity in ferrocement. Those are different targets requiring their own ground truth and validation.
./report_v2/thesis/chapters/02_background.tex:16:Frozen residual-network embeddings provide one intermediate representation between colour rules and fully task-specific deep learning. Residual networks were developed to facilitate the optimization of deep visual models \citep{he2016}; in this activity, an existing network supplies fixed image features and a separately trained regression head predicts the visible label. The saved experiment is evidence about that particular transfer-learning arrangement, not about the universal superiority of deep or handcrafted representations.
./report_v2/thesis/chapters/02_background.tex:26:The evidence reviewed above motivates a specific contribution: testing whether visible-corrosion representations support incremental structural prediction under realistic information and grouping constraints. The study supplies a bounded empirical case, not a new general-purpose model. Its value lies in explaining how apparently encouraging pooled scores change when the target, observation unit, and evaluation population are specified. The next chapter makes that observation structure explicit.

""main_4/outputs/ultimate_load_refocus/pooled_post_onset/grouped_cv/selected_best_model_per_feature_set.csv"", ""report_v2/evidence/v3/figure_checks.json""], ""2"": ""main_4/outputs/ultimate_load_refocus/mesh_stratified/mesh_feature_set_comparison.csv""]}","An accurate visual corrosion model need not provide a transferable estimate of structural capacity. This study examines that boundary using saved experiments on 48 ferrocement specimens from two campaigns. The aligned photographic record contains 791 observations, while wire-area loss and ultimate load are each measured once per specimen at terminal testing. Campaign identity coincides with mesh configuration, chloride concentration, and terminal exposure. A focused terminal-load comparison uses specimen-disjoint, repeated five-fold evaluation and image-only, metadata-only, and combined feature families. Metadata-only Ridge achieves mean terminal-test MAE of 0.173\,kN with fold standard deviation 0.046\,kN. The tested image descriptors do not establish a reliable pooled improvement. A post-onset subset yields a limited MAE gain for a combined model without uniformly better rank correlation. Every selected feature-family model has negative mean test $R^2$ within each mesh group. Campaign exclusion provides a severe extrapolation stress test rather than a representative estimate of deployment error. The contribution is a bounded empirical account of how supervision, metadata controls, and evaluation regime constrain structural claims from images. Model-derived threshold analysis is not validated remaining-life prediction, and no performance result exists for the separate prepared four-class classifier."

[""main/reports/current_corrosion_metrics.csv"", ""main_4/outputs/data/master_table.csv"", ""main_4/outputs/diagnostics/tables/benchmark_best_model_robustness.csv | main_3/outputs/corrosion_regression_metrics.csv | main_3/outputs/damage_regression_metrics.csv"", ""main_4/outputs/models/proxy_rul/proxy_rul_estimates.csv"", ""main_4/outputs/ultimate_load_refocus/mesh_stratified/mesh_feature_set_comparison.csv""]}","An accurate visual corrosion model need not provide a transferable estimate of structural capacity. This study examines that boundary using saved experiments on 48 ferrocement specimens from two campaigns. The aligned photographic record contains 791 observations, while wire-area loss and ultimate load are each measured once per specimen at terminal testing. Campaign identity coincides with mesh configuration, chloride concentration, and terminal exposure. A focused terminal-load comparison uses specimen-disjoint, repeated five-fold evaluation and image-only, metadata-only, and combined feature families. Scoring uses held-out terminal images, rather than a fixed early horizon. Metadata-only Ridge achieves mean terminal-test MAE of 0.173\,kN with fold standard deviation 0.046\,kN. The tested image descriptors do not establish a reliable pooled improvement. A post-onset subset yields a limited MAE gain for a combined model without uniformly better rank correlation. Every selected feature-family model has negative mean test $R^2$ within each mesh group. Campaign exclusion provides a severe extrapolation stress test rather than a representative estimate of deployment error. The contribution is a bounded empirical account of how supervision, metadata controls, and evaluation regime constrain structural claims from images. Model-derived threshold analysis is not validated remaining-life prediction, and no performance result exists for the separate prepared four-class classifier."

The first repeats the comparison within each mesh family, leaving 24 independent structural specimens per family and only four or five terminal test specimens per fold. The second retains observations at and after a specimen's first recorded total-rust value of at least 0.5\%, leaving 43 specimens with eligible terminal observations and eight or nine terminal test specimens per fold. The threshold is a pragmatic sensitivity cutoff. The code retains all later observations once onset occurs; it does not require each later value to remain above the cutoff."

./main_4/FIXES_APPLIED.md:12:4. Rewrite `SCIENTIFIC_REPORT.md` so the written claims match the evidence and limitations.
./REPORT_PLANNING.md:5:**Audit method:** Five parallel deep-dive investigations (main_first+main, main_2, main_3, main_4+main_4_old, out+augmentation+Data+Documentation) plus direct verification of git history, file mtimes, config integrity, workbook contents, and several specific numeric claims by the coordinating session (a revision pass performed after the first audit draft, prompted by inconsistencies the user asked to have re-checked). Every claim below is tagged by evidence tier per the brief's authority hierarchy:
./REPORT_PLANNING.md:6:**(A)** executable code/config actually run · **(B)** generated metrics/tables/manifests/logs · **(C)** associated report/paper · **(D)** descriptive documentation internal to this project · **(P)** predecessor/external experimental documentation (the earlier MSc thesis and the ARTISTE conference paper — these describe the original physical experiments but were not produced by this project and are never treated as this project's own output) · **(E)** inferred/assumption. Repository-navigation files (e.g. `CLAUDE.md`) are used only to orient the audit, never cited as scientific evidence for a claim in this document or in the eventual report — every scientific claim below is grounded in (A)/(B)/(C)/(P). Where sources disagree, both values are kept and the disagreement is logged in Section 4 rather than silently resolved.
./REPORT_PLANNING.md:12:The repository's scientific/implementation work is organized as **six phases**: an informal exploratory prototype (`main_first`), a benchmarked classical-ML baseline (`main`), a deep-image-embedding experiment (`main_2`), an interpretable-feature pipeline linking corrosion to structural condition and proxy-RUL (`main_3`), a robustness-diagnostic-refinement-then-refocus phase (`main_4`, itself two sub-phases: 4a diagnostic refinement, 4b terminal ultimate-load refocus), and a later, methodologically separate four-class visible-corrosion-classification/augmentation effort (`augmentation/` + `Documentation/augmentation_*`). **`out/` is not a research phase** — it is a dissemination layer of condensed IEEE-style papers that repackage main_3/main_4 results (not independent analysis, with one apparent-but-resolved exception — see §4). `Data/` and `Documentation/` hold the master workbook/images plus a large set of project-generated planning documents, and — critically — **two genuine external sources, evidence category P (predecessor/external documentation)**: an earlier MSc thesis by a different student (Al Amin Hossain, s313940) and a 2025 conference paper (ARTISTE), both describing the original physical experimental campaigns that this project builds on and partially reproduces. These are cited where they establish facts about the physical experiment (e.g. the definition of `ultimate_load_kn`, see §4/§8) but are never treated as this project's own output.
./REPORT_PLANNING.md:14:Git history is **not usable for fine-grained chronology**: the entire repo (all `main_*` generations) landed in one commit, `4ba4a65 "Add project files"` (2026-04-08), preceded only by two generic init commits. The `augmentation/` work is the only part with organic incremental commits (`f0a3253` 2026-07-06, `e1e88fb` 2026-09-10). File mtimes are more informative but must be used cautiously: a cluster of files across *every* generation (`main_first`, `main`, `main_2`, `main_3`, `main_4`, `main_4_old`) share the identical modification timestamp 2026-04-01 16:58. **Historically**, the pipeline's configuration files held valid numeric values — this is evidenced by successful, dated output artifacts that could only have been produced with working numeric config (e.g. `main_4/outputs/features/image_features.csv`, dated 2026-03-16; `run_ultimate_load_refocus.log`, dated 2026-03-25). **Currently**, four YAML config files in `main_4`/`main_4_old` contain a corruption: every literal numeral `1` inside a YAML value has been replaced with the string `main_first` (e.g. `reg_lambda: 1.0` → `reg_lambda: main_first.0`), which would break re-execution of the affected code paths today. The repo's squashed git history contains no pre-corruption diff, so the **mechanism is inferred, not proven**: the coincidence in timing with the cross-generation 2026-04-01 16:58 touch is circumstantial, consistent with (but not proof of) a single automated bulk text-replacement operation. This is treated strictly as a Software-Engineering/Reproducibility finding (§ Ch.7) and flagged as an open question in §8 — it is not used as evidence for any scientific conclusion.
./REPORT_PLANNING.md:18:The scientific arc **is** evidence-supported, with important corrections to the brief's proposed story (see §7): main_3 already implemented GroupShuffleSplit/LOTO/LOCO and already *named* campaign confounding and metadata-shortcut learning in its own report — main_4 did not discover these problems, it **quantified and acted on** problems main_3 had already flagged (a nuance the final report must get right). The single strongest, most defensible finding in the whole project is main_4's ultimate-load-refocus mesh-stratification result: a pooled corrosion-vs-load correlation (Spearman ρ=0.462, p≈0.001) **weakens or reverses** once stratified by steel-mesh family (mesh_4: ρ=−0.046; mesh_7: ρ=−0.40) — evidence of substantial confounding by specimen-group structure that prevents the pooled correlation from being read as a direct corrosion-capacity relationship, and the closest thing the project has to a decisive negative-result-turned-insight. This finding concerns the **structural** endpoint specifically; the **visible-corrosion** stage, by contrast, remains robust under the same leave-one-campaign-out evaluation (Spearman ≥0.97 for both surface targets, see §4/§7) — the collapse is specific to hidden structural condition, not a general failure of campaign generalization.
./REPORT_PLANNING.md:51:4. **Phase 3 — Interpretable corrosion → structural condition → proxy-RUL (`main_3`).** *Research question:* using genuinely interpretable, multi-family handcrafted features (91 features: color/HSV/Lab, GLCM/LBP texture, morphology, spatial/strip-position) and specimen-grouped, treatment-grouped, and campaign-grouped splits, can visible corrosion be linked to hidden structural condition and a proxy-RUL? *Result:* surface corrosion regression works well in-distribution (MAE 1.38 for total rust); hidden-damage regression is weak (`wire_area_loss_pct` R²=0.31, `ultimate_load_kn` R²=0.385, RandomForest MAE 0.121 kN on this generation's own 38/10 grouped-holdout split) and **already visibly unstable under LOTO** (R² swinging from +0.76 to −50.75 across treatment folds); the project's own report already names campaign confounding and a "downstream label shortcut" (manual corrosion labels used as damage-model inputs) as concrete technical risks; correlation between visible corrosion and wire loss is near-zero (r=0.021). Proxy-RUL is computed and consistently never mislabeled as true RUL. *Limitation discovered (explicit, in the project's own words):* "Small structural supervision… is the dominant bottleneck"; hidden-damage predictions used to build degradation curves are in-sample fits, not held-out evidence. *Why the next phase started:* main_3 itself specifies almost the entire main_4 agenda in writing (ablations isolating metadata vs. image signal, out-of-fold estimates, monotonic degradation constraints) — main_4 operationalizes fixes for problems main_3 already named, rather than discovering new ones.
./REPORT_PLANNING.md:55:6. **Phase 4b — Terminal ultimate-load refocus (`main_4`, second half).** *Research question (explicit, from config/README):* "After controlling for specimen design, does superficial corrosion add predictive value for terminal ultimate load?" *Result:* metadata-only Ridge is the best and most robust model (MAE 0.173 kN, Spearman 0.758); adding RGB/HSV image descriptors yields no meaningful improvement pooled, and the pooled corrosion-vs-load correlation (ρ=0.462) **weakens or reverses once stratified by mesh family** (mesh_4: ρ=−0.046; mesh_7: ρ=−0.40) — evidence of substantial confounding by specimen-group structure that prevents the pooled correlation from being read as evidence of a direct corrosion-capacity relationship (not proof that no such relationship could exist under other conditions). *Limitation discovered:* under LOCO, all feature sets still collapse (best MAE 0.465 kN vs. 0.173 baseline) — refocusing the target did not remove the campaign-extrapolation problem, only made the in-distribution finding more defensible. *Status:* the legacy hidden-damage/degradation/proxy-RUL pipeline is retained for traceability but explicitly demoted to non-preferred in the current `README.md`.
./REPORT_PLANNING.md:79:| 11 | Chronology signal reliability | Folder-name order implies `main_first` is chronologically first, all generations built sequentially over months | File mtimes show `main_first`'s own scripts (Apr 1, 2026) postdating parts of `main` (Mar 6-17, 2026), and a cluster of files across every generation sharing the identical mtime Apr 1 16:58; entire repo landed in one git commit | mtime evidence + git log | Consistent with a repository reorganization/bulk-copy event (possibly assembling scattered local work into one tracked repo, or an automated tool pass) rather than organic sequential authorship — the specific mechanism is not provable from available evidence | State chronology as supported by code/content maturity and explicit cross-references between phases (the primary evidence), not by raw filesystem timestamps; flag mtime-based ordering as corroborating only |
./REPORT_PLANNING.md:91:- Evidence: `main` through `main_4` README/PDF abstracts, `Documentation/Thesis/` and `Documentation/Conferences/` (evidence category P — cited, not reproduced), IEEE paper abstracts. (`CLAUDE.md` may be consulted informally to navigate the repository while drafting, but is not cited as evidence for any claim in the written report — see evidence-tier note at the top of this document.)
./REPORT_PLANNING.md:178:`main_4` took these self-diagnosed weaknesses and quantified them rigorously, and the quantification revealed something specific: it is the **structural** targets (`wire_area_loss_frac`, `ultimate_load_kn`) that collapse under leave-one-campaign-out evaluation — ultimate-load MAE inflated 3.24× relative to a standard grouped holdout — while the **visible-corrosion** targets (`surface_total_rust_pct`, `peak_rust_pct`) retain Spearman correlations at or above 0.97 under the same campaign-holdout evaluation. Feature-importance and robustness analysis further showed that whatever robust structural signal existed came predominantly from experimental-design metadata (mesh count, treatment, NaCl level) rather than from the corrosion images. Rather than treating the structural collapse as a modeling problem to be engineered around, the project made a defensible scientific decision to refocus: instead of trying to predict a sparse, campaign-confounded hidden-damage proxy or an unreachable "true" remaining-useful-life, it re-targeted the one directly measured, physically meaningful terminal endpoint available — the terminal ultimate load recorded at the end of each specimen's aging period (defined, per the predecessor thesis, as the peak load recorded during a four-point bending test) — and explicitly tested, rather than assumed, whether visible corrosion adds predictive value once specimen design is controlled for. The resulting analysis is the project's most scientifically informative finding: a pooled correlation between visible corrosion and terminal ultimate load that looks statistically real (ρ=0.462) weakens or reverses once the data is stratified by steel-mesh family (mesh_4: ρ=−0.046; mesh_7: ρ=−0.40). This indicates substantial confounding by specimen-group structure and prevents the pooled correlation from being interpreted as evidence of a direct corrosion-capacity relationship — it does not, on its own, prove the pooled relationship is purely an artifact with no underlying corrosion effect, only that the pooled evidence cannot support such a claim. This is precisely the failure mode the project had been warning about in its own documentation since main_3.
./REPORT_PLANNING.md:180:The strongest current, evidence-backed conclusions are therefore modest but genuine, and depend on which endpoint is being discussed: (1) visible surface corrosion can be quantified from images with good in-distribution accuracy, and this accuracy is largely retained under campaign-holdout evaluation — the visible-corrosion stage is the project's most robust result; (2) prediction of hidden structural condition (wire-area loss, terminal ultimate load) from the same images is comparatively weak and collapses under campaign-holdout evaluation specifically, with metadata describing specimen design proving a more robust — though scientifically less interesting — predictor of terminal structural condition than anything derived from the corrosion images; and (3) any claim that visible corrosion predicts hidden structural loss or remaining service life must be qualified by the sparse, single-terminal-measurement, campaign-confounded nature of the available structural ground truth. Two matters remain open rather than concluded: a separate, later-started effort to build a robust four-class visible-corrosion-severity classifier has completed careful, leakage-safe data preparation and a tailored augmentation methodology but has not yet trained or evaluated a classification model (explicitly deferred to a planned future paper); and the repository currently contains a reproducibility defect in several `main_4` configuration files (numeric values replaced by a text string, cause unproven) that would need to be repaired before the refocus pipeline's exact numbers could be regenerated from scratch — a software-engineering caveat about re-running the pipeline today, not a challenge to the validity of the previously generated outputs and reports, which remain valid evidence of what was found.

Instead of asking whether images can predict full structural life, we ask whether they can improve the estimation of a directly measured terminal quantity: ultimate load. This reframing is central to the paper. It aligns the learning task with the strongest available structural evidence and allows a clearer comparison between engineering metadata and image-derived corrosion descriptors.

""main_4/outputs/ultimate_load_refocus/mesh_stratified/mesh_feature_set_comparison.csv""]}","An accurate visual corrosion model need not provide a transferable estimate of structural capacity. This study examines that boundary using saved experiments on 48 ferrocement specimens from two campaigns. The aligned photographic record contains 791 observations, while wire-area loss and ultimate load are each measured once per specimen at terminal testing. Campaign identity coincides with mesh configuration, chloride concentration, and terminal exposure. A focused terminal-load comparison uses specimen-disjoint, repeated five-fold evaluation and image-only, metadata-only, and combined feature families. Metadata-only Ridge achieves mean terminal-test MAE of 0.173\,kN with fold standard deviation 0.046\,kN. The tested image descriptors do not establish a reliable pooled improvement. A post-onset subset yields a limited MAE gain for a combined model without uniformly better rank correlation. Every selected feature-family model has negative mean test $R^2$ within each mesh group. Campaign exclusion provides a severe extrapolation stress test rather than a representative estimate of deployment error. The contribution is a bounded empirical account of how supervision, metadata controls, and evaluation regime constrain structural claims from images. Model-derived threshold analysis is not validated remaining-life prediction, and no performance result exists for the separate prepared four-class classifier."

""main_4/outputs/ultimate_load_refocus/pooled_all_weeks/leave_one_campaign_out/feature_set_comparison.csv"", ""main_4/outputs/ultimate_load_refocus/pooled_post_onset/grouped_cv/selected_best_model_per_feature_set.csv"", ""report_v2/tables/loco_capacity.csv""], ""2"": [""main/reports/current_corrosion_metrics.csv"", ""main_4/outputs/data/master_table.csv"", ""main_4/outputs/diagnostics/tables/benchmark_best_model_robustness.csv | ""main_4/outputs/ultimate_load_refocus/mesh_stratified/mesh_feature_set_comparison.csv""]}","An accurate visual corrosion model need not provide a transferable estimate of structural capacity. This study examines that boundary using saved experiments on 48 ferrocement specimens from two campaigns. The aligned photographic record contains 791 observations, while wire-area loss and ultimate load are each measured once per specimen at terminal testing. Campaign identity coincides with mesh configuration, chloride concentration, and terminal exposure. A focused terminal-load comparison uses specimen-disjoint, repeated five-fold evaluation and image-only, metadata-only, and combined feature families. Scoring uses held-out terminal images, rather than a fixed early horizon. Metadata-only Ridge achieves mean terminal-test MAE of 0.173\,kN with fold standard deviation 0.046\,kN. The tested image descriptors do not establish a reliable pooled improvement. A post-onset subset yields a limited MAE gain for a combined model without uniformly better rank correlation. Every selected feature-family model has negative mean test $R^2$ within each mesh group. Campaign exclusion provides a severe extrapolation stress test rather than a representative estimate of deployment error. The contribution is a bounded empirical account of how supervision, metadata controls, and evaluation regime constrain structural claims from images. Model-derived threshold analysis is not validated remaining-life prediction, and no performance result exists for the separate prepared four-class classifier."

./Documentation/augmentation_phase1_intent.md:11:The advisor is not primarily asking for augmentation as a technique. They are asking for augmentation as an enabling step toward a specific comparison: **a deep learning model vs. the threshold-based red-color classification approach**. The core goal is to produce evidence that a learned representation (DL) outperforms a rule-based one (color thresholding), and augmentation is the mechanism proposed to make the DL model viable on a small dataset.

./Documentation/augmentation_journal_plan.md:301:| Risk | Removes real rust pixels from the image while the class label (e.g., "moderate rust") stays unchanged. If the erased region contains the primary visual evidence for the class, the model sees contradictory training data. The risk is highest for specimens in the mild and moderate severity classes, which have limited rust coverage. |
./Documentation/augmentation_methodology_final.md:187:### Parameter ranges and their evidence basis
./Documentation/project_review.md:46:No uncertainty quantification (point estimates only), no mechanistic or physics-informed degradation model, no multi-task learning across structural targets, no Bayesian treatment, no ablation of proxy-RUL threshold choices against failure evidence, no mixed-effects model for specimen-level random variation, no cross-specimen temporal modeling.
./Documentation/project_review.md:117:`surface_total_rust_pct` in the workbook is numerically computed from images using the same RGB threshold used by the current pipeline. The "prediction" of this label from image features is circular: the model is predicting the output of a deterministic function from its inputs. This result must not be presented as evidence that corrosion can be estimated from images.
./Documentation/project_review.md:228:**Why it is likely to fail:** (1) 791 images across 48 specimens is too small for self-supervised visual representation learning to produce useful embeddings. (2) The structural label bottleneck (48 rows) means any learned representation has only 48 supervision signals to calibrate against. (3) The existing finding that image features do not outperform metadata in a well-regularized 8-feature model is strong evidence against richer image representations helping structural targets.
./Documentation/project_review.md:253:| Use isotonic regression degradation as evidence of learned degradation dynamics | It is monotone interpolation, not a physical model; it cannot extrapolate |
./Documentation/augmentation_objective_decision.md:135:**What this means:** The professor's stated goal. Produce experimental evidence that a DL model outperforms a fixed threshold approach for corrosion surface quantification.
./Documentation/augmentation_objective_decision.md:182:**What success means:** An augmented DL embedding improves `ultimate_load_kn` prediction under **grouped 5-fold CV** (not LOCO). Success under LOCO would require the corrosion signal to transfer across campaign boundaries, which the existing evidence suggests it does not.
./Documentation/final_strategy.md:44:**Recommendation:** Restructure the paper abstract and introduction so that the central claim is: *"We demonstrate, under experimentally realistic campaign holdout, that interpretable image features from repeated surface photographs do not contribute structural prognostic value beyond specimen design metadata in ferrocement corrosion assessment."* Every subsequent section should be positioned as evidence for or context around this claim.
./Documentation/final_strategy.md:46:**Why it is valuable:** The current framing leads with the pipeline and ends with weak results. A reviewer reading in this order experiences disappointment. Reversing the order — leading with the finding, then showing the evidence — produces a coherent argument. Negative results with rigorous evaluation and a clear mechanistic explanation are publishable and citable. A pipeline paper with ambiguous results is neither.
./Documentation/final_strategy.md:48:**Scientific problem it addresses:** The paper currently lacks a falsifiable central claim. "We built a pipeline" is not a scientific claim. "Image features do not predict structural damage under campaign holdout, because campaign is confounded with mesh design" is a falsifiable claim with strong evidence.
./Documentation/final_strategy.md:56:**Reviewer impact:** Very high. A clearly stated negative finding with rigorous evidence is far easier to accept than a positive claim with weak evidence.
./Documentation/final_strategy.md:250:If the linear model matches or beats RandomForest: the paper's conclusion is strengthened — ML adds nothing over a two-covariate linear model. If the linear model is worse: the paper has a new piece of evidence that the tree-based model does capture some non-linear structure even under LOCO.
./Documentation/final_strategy.md:346:**Self-supervised learning or contrastive pretraining.** 791 images is below the minimum practical size for self-supervised visual representation learning. Even if a useful embedding were learned, it would still face the same 48-sample structural-label ceiling and the same campaign confounding under LOCO. The verification audit shows no evidence that richer representations have been tried and failed, but the structural argument is clear: the limit is the experimental design, not the image representation. Stop.
./Documentation/final_strategy.md:350:**Additional feature engineering.** The current pipeline extracts 77 interpretable features covering rust mask area, color histograms (RGB and HSV), GLCM texture, LBP texture, rust blob morphology, and longitudinal strip statistics. The verification audit found 36 high-collinearity feature pairs already pruned and 13 near-constant features excluded. The feature space is mature. The finding that `metadata_only` outperforms `all_cleaned` and `image_time_only` under LOCO is strong evidence that the problem is not feature richness. Additional features will add noise, not signal. Stop.

./main_4/main.tex:238:\textbf{Input.} Parsed metadata from the workbook audit stage. \textbf{Output.} An enriched master table with validated specimen mapping, cleaned numeric columns, and structural-row flags. \textbf{Purpose.} This step turns the workbook into a modelling-ready longitudinal table without losing audit traceability. \textbf{Key design choices.} A deterministic mapping file, \texttt{configs/specimen\_mapping.yaml}, reconstructs \texttt{campaign\_id}, \texttt{series\_id}, \texttt{split\_group\_treatment}, \texttt{treatment\_protocol}, terminal duration, and campaign-linked specimen attributes. The code also standardizes \texttt{cover\_mm}, forces \texttt{n\_steel\_mesh} to integer type, creates \texttt{has\_structural\_label}, and resolves a source inconsistency by treating the workbook field \texttt{Last\_Wire\_Area\_Loss\_(Faliure\_Surface)\_\%} as a fraction-valued variable, saved canonically as \texttt{wire\_area\_loss\_frac} and secondarily as \texttt{wire\_area\_loss\_pct}. \textbf{Residual limitations.} Campaign and treatment assignments are reconstructed from thesis evidence rather than directly encoded in the workbook, so the mapping is deterministic and reviewable but not fully self-contained in the raw data.

./main_3/reports/corrosion_condition_pipeline_ieee.tex:88:A multi-stage paper becomes hard to interpret unless the evidence status of each quantity is explicit. ./main_3/reports/corrosion_condition_pipeline_ieee.tex:92:    \label{tab:evidence-structure}
./main_3/reports/corrosion_condition_pipeline_ieee.tex:288:The hidden-damage stage shows learnable signal, but it is substantially weaker than the visible-corrosion stage. In the grouped specimen-holdout split, the best model for wire-area loss is Random Forest with MAE = 3.90, RMSE = 5.00, and $R^2 = 0.310$. For ultimate load, the best saved MAE is 0.121~kN from Random Forest, although Extra Trees reaches a slightly higher $R^2$ of 0.418 with worse MAE (0.145~kN). These results are best interpreted as feasibility evidence, not as robust structural prediction.
./main_3/reports/corrosion_condition_pipeline_ieee.tex:309:The stricter holdouts make the evidence hierarchy much clearer. For visible corrosion, leave-one-campaign-out still retains positive $R^2$: the best mean results are MAE = 2.10 and $R^2 = 0.582$ for total rust (Random Forest) and MAE = 6.21 with $R^2 = 0.595$ for peak rust (HistGradientBoosting). However, leave-one-treatment-out is much less stable: the best mean $R^2$ becomes negative for both visible corrosion targets, even when MAE remains moderate.
./main_3/reports/corrosion_condition_pipeline_ieee.tex:384:The main achievement of the study is therefore not a single score, but a coherent corrosion-to-condition workflow with clear evidence boundaries: direct surface evidence first, mixed-input latent structural estimation second, empirical degradation fitting third, and screening-only outputs last.
./main_3/reports/corrosion_condition_pipeline_ieee.tex:402:The strongest validated evidence lies in the visible-corrosion stage. The hidden-damage stage remains feasible but structurally weak, and the proxy-RUL layer is screening-only by design. The most defensible summary is therefore simple: repeated corrosion images support a useful corrosion-to-condition workflow, but sparse structural supervision sharply limits what can be claimed about hidden damage and remaining life.

./main_3/main.aux:90:\newlabel{fig:damage-parity}{{3}{24}{Saved parity plot for damage regression under \texttt {group\_shuffle}. The plot is useful as an interpolation diagnostic only; it should not be read as cross-campaign or treatment-robust evidence}{figure.caption.11}{}}
./main_3/main.tex:48:This report documents what the repository state of \texttt{main\_3} actually implements. The evidence base is the executable code in \texttt{src/} and \texttt{scripts/}, the configuration in \texttt{configs/default.toml}, the saved tables and figures in \texttt{outputs/} and \texttt{reports/}, the pipeline logs in \texttt{logs/}, and the thesis and conference PDFs stored in \texttt{../Documentation}. The implemented system reconstructs a canonical specimen-time dataset, extracts classical image descriptors from 792 PNG images, trains image-only models for visible corrosion targets, trains mixed-input regressors for structural proxy targets on only 48 late-stage rows, fits specimen-wise degradation curves, and estimates threshold-based proxy remaining useful life (RUL). The repository does not support direct supervised image-to-RUL learning, and the downstream hidden-damage/RUL stages are not end-to-end image-only because the saved damage models use manual corrosion labels and metadata as inputs. All conclusions below are therefore limited to what this implemented pipeline can defensibly support.
./main_3/main.tex:425:Two methodological caveats are essential. First, the final state-estimate tables are generated by refitting the selected model on all available structural rows and then predicting all 792 rows. Consequently, structural predictions for the 48 labeled rows are in-sample fits, not held-out validation evidence. Second, the damage stage is not a pure image model; it explicitly uses manual corrosion labels and campaign-linked metadata.
./main_3/main.tex:579:\caption{Saved parity plot for damage regression under \texttt{group\_shuffle}. The plot is useful as an interpolation diagnostic only; it should not be read as cross-campaign or treatment-robust evidence.}
./main_3/main.tex:583:The saved full-data damage models also indicate that the two structural targets draw signal from different sources. For wire loss, the largest random-forest importances are \texttt{peak\_rust\_pct\_feature} (0.111), \texttt{cover\_failure\_surface\_mm} (0.093), \texttt{lab\_a\_mean} (0.081), \texttt{rust\_component\_count} (0.076), and manual \texttt{peak\_rust\_location\_cm} (0.075). For ultimate load, the leading importances are \texttt{n\_steel\_mesh} (0.172), both one-hot campaign indicators (0.128 and 0.105), \texttt{week} (0.101), \texttt{ageing\_days} (0.095), and \texttt{nacl\_pct} (0.082). This means the current load model is dominated by campaign-linked metadata rather than purely by image evidence.
./main_3/main.tex:643:  \item the saved hidden-damage models use metadata and manual corrosion labels, not just image-derived evidence.
./main_3/main.tex:646:This means the current damage stage is estimating latent structural state from a mixed evidence bundle, not discovering a purely image-based structural law.
./main_3/main.tex:744:The repository does not contain a clean ablation study, so the exact fraction cannot be recovered from saved outputs alone. However, the saved full ultimate-load random forest is dominated by metadata and campaign indicators, which is strong evidence that metadata contributes materially.
